15.10  The 14-Lab Evolution
Each lab is a working increment that adds one capability to this worklet and activates specific nodes. Ship the deliverable and pass acceptance before advancing — by Lab 14 they compose into the deployed decision brief with options and risks system.

Lab 1 — Agent vs Chatbot — the decision brief with options and risks planner loop
Phase: A · Foundations     Goal: Establish the agent loop (clarify → plan → revise) instead of a one-shot chatbot answer.
What it adds to this worklet.  A minimal planner that clarifies the request, proposes a plan for producing the decision brief with options and risks, and revises on feedback — the seed this worklet grows from.
Nodes activated.  Coordinator loop (no specialist nodes yet).
Pitfall to avoid.  Don't answer in one shot — without clarify/revise it's a chatbot, not an agent.
Lab 1 — plan & revise

Lab 2 — Tool-Using Agent — call data & compute
Phase: A · Foundations     Goal: Give the agent tools it must call to act, rather than answering from memory.
What it adds to this worklet.  Readers for institutional KPIs & dashboards and department reports, plus a domain calculator/checker, so the planner acts on real data.
Nodes activated.  Tool layer feeding the early data agents.
Pitfall to avoid.  Never assert a fact in the prompt that a tool should verify.
Lab 2 — typed tools

Lab 3 — Skill Creation — reusable plan & format skills
Phase: A · Foundations     Goal: Package repeatable capability as reusable, schema-typed skills.
What it adds to this worklet.  A plan/blueprint skill and a formatting skill that renders the decision brief with options and risks — both reusable across cases.
Nodes activated.  Plan node + Assembly node (as skills).
Pitfall to avoid.  Keep skills case-agnostic and typed; hard-coding one case breaks reuse.
Lab 3 — skill interface

Lab 4 — Memory & Retrieval — state vs stored
Phase: A · Foundations     Goal: Separate short-term run state from long-term stored knowledge.
What it adds to this worklet.  Session state (the current draft) vs stored reference — institutional KPIs & dashboards and the history of past decision / KPIs — retrieved by tags and by similarity.
Nodes activated.  Retrieval node; seeds validation/no-repeat.
Pitfall to avoid.  Don't conflate run state with the archive — mixing them corrupts the baseline.
Lab 4 — memory split

Lab 5 — MCP-Style Connector — one server for all sources
Phase: A · Foundations     Goal: Expose every data source through one typed connector.
What it adds to this worklet.  A single connector fronting institutional KPIs & dashboards, department reports and external benchmarks so every agent reaches data the same governed way.
Nodes activated.  Connector under the data agents.
Pitfall to avoid.  No agent should touch a source directly; a bypass defeats governance later.
Lab 5 — connector

Lab 6 — Runtime — skills, tools, memory, audit
Phase: A · Foundations     Goal: Run the agent inside a controlled runtime with a full audit log.
What it adds to this worklet.  A runtime binding skills + tools + memory behind the loop, enforcing budgets and approval gates and logging every step — the backbone later labs plug into.
Nodes activated.  Coordinator runtime around all nodes.
Pitfall to avoid.  Log inputs and outputs, not just messages; checkpoint before risky steps or resume loses state.

15.11  Reference Architecture, Pilot & MVP
Reference architecture
Data capture: department academic, research, placement, and finance systems (read access).
Data layer: institution-wide schema for domain metrics, KPI definitions, targets, and evidence.
AI layer: cross-department aggregation, KPI synthesis, variance analysis, report generation.
Workflow layer: Registrar-authorized access, Principal/Registrar review queue, dashboard, Council distribution.
Governance layer: read-access control, no-forecast-as-final rule, drill-down traceability, audit log.
Pilot artifacts
An institution-level read-access connector to each domain system for one review cycle.
A KPI synthesis engine with approved NIRF/NAAC target definitions.
A report-generation pipeline with drill-down links.
An executive dashboard with an exportable leadership report.
Suggested MVP roadmap
Risks & mitigations
15.12  Guardrails, Metrics, Milestones & Glossary
Guardrails
Do not circulate an institution-wide KPI or executive report without Principal/Director/Registrar approval.
Keep department-level detail traceable but access-controlled.
Never present a forecast, provisional figure, or unreconciled number as final.
Use only approved KPI definitions and targets; show the formula and source behind every KPI.
Version every executive report and keep an audit log of circulation.

=== TABLE 1 ===
Phase | What it establishes
Phase A — Foundations (Labs 1–6) | Turn a single agent loop into a tool-using, skill-driven, memory-backed agent that reaches all data through one connector and runs inside a governed runtime with an audit log.
Phase B — Orchestration (Labs 7–8) | Wire the specialist agents into an explicit node graph, then parallelise the per-unit work and merge the best results.
Phase C — Applied reliability (Labs 9–11) | Ground every output in evidence, make the pipeline self-healing on validation failure, and treat the spec as a tested build.
Phase D — Governance & capstone (Labs 12–14) | Add safety, approval, evaluation and rollback; plug in domain specialists; integrate everything into a deployed, team-owned system.


=== TABLE 2 ===
Capability | What it means
Faculty | Faculty loads the course syllabus and the list of lab activities completed before the exam.
The | The Question Generation Agent drafts candidate questions grounded in the covered syllabus and completed activities; faculty selects the questions and sets the maximum marks per question.
The | The Test-Case Intake Agent ingests the faculty-provided test cases (visible + hidden) with expected outputs and per-case weights, mapped to each problem.
The | The Submission Intake Agent accepts each student's soft-copy code, detects the language, and timestamps it against the exam window.
The | The Sandbox Execution Agent compiles and runs each submission against every test case in an isolated, resource-limited sandbox and records pass/fail, runtime, and errors.
The | The Code Similarity Agent flags submissions that are suspiciously similar to one another for faculty review.


=== TABLE 3 ===
# | Agent | Role & communication | Guardrails
0 | apniLeap AURA Coordinator | Receives the faculty/COE prompt command, checks permissions, creates the run plan, initializes agents, and monitors state across setup, execution, and review. | Never set marks or publish without faculty approval; enforce data scope, sandbox isolation, audit logging, and exception routing.
1 | Question Generation Agent | Drafts candidate lab questions grounded in the covered syllabus and completed activities for faculty to choose from. | Draft only from covered syllabus and completed activities; never finalize a question or set marks — faculty choose the questions and the marks.
2 | Test-Case Intake Agent | Ingests the faculty-provided visible and hidden test cases, expected outputs, and per-case weights, and maps them to each question. | Use only faculty-provided test cases; never invent, modify, or drop a test case; keep hidden test cases confidential.
3 | Submission Intake Agent | Accepts each student's soft-copy code, detects the language, and timestamps it against the exam window. | Accept only within the exam window; never alter a submission or its timestamp; keep each submission private to its student.
4 | Sandbox Execution Agent | Compiles and runs each submission against every test case in an isolated sandbox; records pass/fail, runtime, and errors. | Run only in a network-disabled, resource- and time-limited sandbox; never execute code outside it; never expose one student's code to another.
5 | Code Similarity Agent | Flags submissions that are suspiciously similar to one another for faculty review. | Flag for human review only; never auto-penalize or reduce marks; cite the compared submissions.
6 | Scoring Agent | Computes marks from the weights of passed test cases, capped at the faculty-assigned maximum for the question. | Never award more than the faculty-assigned maximum; never count a failing case as passed; show the per-test-case marks breakdown.
7 | Feedback (Where-You-Went-Wrong) Agent | Drafts a feedback report — failing test cases, expected versus actual output, compile/runtime errors, and the likely root cause. | Draft only from actual execution evidence; never fabricate a diagnosis; do not reveal hidden test-case internals beyond what faculty permit.
8 | Faculty Review | Reviews flagged, borderline, and high-similarity submissions; approves or adjusts marks and feedback. | Faculty decision is the final authority on every question, mark, and feedback comment.
9 | Result Publishing & Archive Agent | Publishes approved marks and feedback and archives the submission, execution log, and marks record. | Publish only faculty-approved results; keep a full audit log of marks awarded and who approved them.


=== TABLE 4 ===
From | To | Message / payload | When
Coordinator | Question Generation Agent | run-context + task | run start
Question Generation Agent | Test-Case Intake Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Test-Case Intake Agent | Submission Intake Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Submission Intake Agent | Sandbox Execution Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Sandbox Execution Agent | Code Similarity Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Code Similarity Agent | Scoring Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Scoring Agent | Feedback (Where-You-Went-Wrong) Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Feedback (Where-You-Went-Wrong) Agent | Faculty Review | state / partial result | in pipeline
Faculty Review | Result Publishing & Archive Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Result Publishing & Archive Agent | Faculty Examiner gate | draft graded lab submission + evidence | for review
Faculty Examiner gate | Coordinator | approve | edit | override | human decision
Coordinator | Governance / Publish | final graded lab submission (versioned) | on approval


=== TABLE 5 ===
Entity | Key fields
Course context | Course (OS/DBMS/DS/Algorithms/ML/DL/…), syllabus units covered, and lab activities completed before the exam.
Question definition | Faculty-chosen problem statement, difficulty, allowed languages, and maximum marks (faculty choice).
Test-case suite | Faculty-provided visible and hidden test cases with expected outputs and per-case weights, mapped to each question.
Student submission | Student soft-copy code, detected language, and submission timestamp.
Evaluation record | Per-test-case pass/fail, computed marks (capped at the maximum), similarity flag, and where-you-went-wrong feedback, pending faculty approval.


=== TABLE 6 ===
function run_programming_lab_examination_auto_evaluat(request):
    ctx = Coordinator.new_run(request)        # isolated, checkpointed
    plan = Coordinator.build_plan(request)     # locked before work begins
    ctx = QuestionGenerationAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = TestCaseIntakeAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = SubmissionIntakeAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = SandboxExecutionAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = CodeSimilarityAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ScoringAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = Feedback.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = FacultyReview.step(ctx, plan)
    # … remaining specialist agents run in order
    report = ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan)   # hard gate
    while not report.passed and attempts < MAX:    # self-healing (Lab 10)
        Coordinator.regenerate(report.failing); report = validate(ctx, plan)
    draft = assemble(ctx)                          # the graded lab submission
    decision = FacultyGate.review(draft)  # HUMAN — final authority
    if decision.approved:
        final = apply(decision.edits, draft)
        Governance.publish(final, version=next()); Audit.log_all()
    return final


=== TABLE 7 ===
function ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan):
    checks = [
      ('awarded marks never exceed the faculty maximum', test_0(ctx, plan)),
      ('every test case runs inside the sandbox', test_1(ctx, plan)),
      ('feedback cites the exact failing case', test_2(ctx, plan)),
      ('no student code executes outside the sandbox', test_3(ctx, plan)),
    ]
    failing = [n for n,ok in checks if not ok]
    return Report(passed = not failing, failing = failing,
                  diagnosis = explain(failing))


=== TABLE 8 ===
connector.expose:
    read('the question & test-case bank')
    read('student code submissions')
    read('the rubric & marks scheme')
agents.use(connector)   # no agent touches a source directly


=== TABLE 9 ===
Stage | What happens
Plan / slot | The coordinator locks the plan and picks one student submission to work — its constraints fixed before any content is chosen.
Source candidate | A retrieval agent proposes a candidate for this student submission from the question & test-case bank, with provenance.
Grounded candidate | A generation agent produces a fresh candidate grounded in the faculty-provided test cases and rubric, attaching an evidence record (source, scope, tags).
Screen & select | Candidates are calibrated and screened; the best-aligned one that satisfies the plan is selected, the rest logged.
Validation | The result is checked (awarded marks never exceed the faculty maximum per question; feedback cites the exact failing case); on failure only this student submission is regenerated.
Human review | Faculty Examiner sees the draft graded lab submission with its evidence and validation report, and approves, edits or overrides.
Publish & audit | On approval the governed publish versions the graded lab submission, records the audit trail, and updates history for future runs.


=== TABLE 10 ===
Stage | Execution mode & coordinator behavior
Gate 1: question & test-case setup | Sequential — faculty selects questions, sets the maximum marks, and approves the test-case suite before the exam opens.
Submission intake | Sequential per student — submissions are accepted only within the exam window and timestamped.
Parallel execution | Parallel across submissions — Sandbox Execution and Code Similarity run per submission as it arrives, each in isolation.
Scoring | Sequential per submission — the Scoring Agent runs after all test cases execute and caps marks at the faculty maximum.
Feedback draft | Parallel across students — the Feedback Agent drafts where-you-went-wrong reports once scoring completes.
Faculty approval | Sequential — marks and feedback are approved before publishing.
Publish & archive | Parallel after approval — approved results are published and archived together.


=== TABLE 11 ===
Panel | Shows
Question & marks setup | Faculty-selected questions with maximum marks and the mapped test-case suite per question.
Live evaluation | Per-student, per-test-case pass/fail with provisional (capped) marks as submissions run.
Scores & flags | Marks distribution with borderline, error, and high-similarity flags for review.
Feedback & results | Approved marks and the where-you-went-wrong report released to each student.


=== TABLE 12 ===
Inputs | Outputs | Agents / nodes | Deliverable & acceptance
Free-text request; core parameters. | A clarified request + an editable outline plan for the graded lab submission. | Coordinator (loop only). | A notebook agent that clarifies, plans and revises a graded lab submission outline.  Accept: Given a vague request it asks at least two clarifying questions and yields a coherent, editable plan.


=== TABLE 13 ===
req  = clarify(user_request)         # ask until inputs complete
plan = propose_plan(req)             # outline for the graded lab submission
while user.wants_changes(plan): plan = revise(plan, user.feedback)
return plan                          # not final — a structured plan

the question & test-case bank; student code submissions; parameters. | Verified computed values and parsed inputs. | Coordinator + tool layer. | An agent that reads the question & test-case bank and computes/validates through typed tools.  Accept: Key numbers and lookups always go through tools, never hand-done in the prompt.

skill plan(inputs) -> Plan:  return expand(inputs).lock()
skill format_graded(result, template) -> Document

Inputs | Outputs | Agents / nodes | Deliverable & acceptance
the question & test-case bank; history of prior student submissions; current draft. | Tag- and similarity-based retrieval. | Retrieval agent; foundations of later screening. | Retrieval over the store + history with tag filters and embedding similarity.  Accept: For a given task, retrieval returns correctly-tagged candidates and similar prior items.


=== TABLE 19 ===
state.draft = candidate                       # short-term

=== TABLE 40 ===
Order | Capability | What it delivers | Agents involved
1 | Question setup | Draft questions from covered syllabus; faculty select and set max marks. | Coordinator, Question Generation Agent.
2 | Test-case intake | Visible + hidden weighted test cases mapped per question. | Test-Case Intake Agent.
3 | Sandbox execution | Compile/run student soft copies against test cases in isolation. | Submission Intake, Sandbox Execution Agents.
4 | Capped scoring | Marks from passed-case weights, never above the faculty maximum. | Scoring Agent.
5 | Feedback + similarity | Where-you-went-wrong report and similarity flags. | Feedback Agent, Code Similarity Agent.
6 | Faculty review & publish | Approve marks/feedback; publish and archive with a dashboard. | Faculty Review, Result Publishing & Archive Agent.


=== TABLE 41 ===
Risk | Why it matters | Mitigation (where handled)
Plan drift | individual results deviate from the locked plan | Lock the plan (Lab 3/7); validation rejects any deviation before the human sees it.
Ungrounded output | content not backed by the faculty-provided test cases and rubric | Grounded generation with an evidence record per unit (Lab 9); ungrounded items are rejected.
Failed checks slip through | a result violates a rule (awarded marks never exceed the faculty maximum per question) | Validation is a hard gate (Lab 10/11); failing results are diagnosed and regenerated, never advanced.
Runaway loops | the regenerate loop never converges | Bound MAX_ATTEMPTS (Lab 7/10); on exhaustion, escalate to faculty examiner with a diagnosis.
Over-automation | the system acts without faculty examiner | The human gate is a hard stop; all outputs are drafts; edits and overrides are effortless and logged (Lab 12).

Week | Labs | Focus | Exit deliverable
Week 1 | Labs 1–3 | Planner loop, tools, reusable skills | Runnable planner + core skill
Week 2 | Labs 4–6 | Memory/retrieval, connector, governed runtime | Data via connector; audited runs
Week 3 | Labs 7–8 | Full node graph + parallel processing | End-to-end draft — shared with faculty
Week 4 | Labs 9–10 | Grounding + self-healing validation | Grounded output; auto-repair on faults
Week 5 | Labs 11–12 | Tested build + safety/governance | Tested artifact; approval, metrics, rollback
Week 6 | Labs 13–14 | Domain specialisation + capstone | Deployed system + demo on a real case

=== TABLE 44 ===
Term | Meaning
Coordinator | The orchestrator agent that owns run-state, plan, budgets and the audit log.
Node graph | The explicit wiring of agents, gates and back-edges for one worklet.
Human gate | The approval step; the named human is the final authority.
Grounding | Linking an output to a real in-scope source (evidence).
Validation | The automated acceptance gate a result must pass before human review.
Blueprint / plan | The locked specification the pipeline obeys.


=== TABLE 45 ===
Phase | What it establishes
Phase A — Foundations (Labs 1–6) | Turn a single agent loop into a tool-using, skill-driven, memory-backed agent that reaches all data through one connector and runs inside a governed runtime with an audit log.
Phase B — Orchestration (Labs 7–8) | Wire the specialist agents into an explicit node graph, then parallelise the per-unit work and merge the best results.
Phase C — Applied reliability (Labs 9–11) | Ground every output in evidence, make the pipeline self-healing on validation failure, and treat the spec as a tested build.
Phase D — Governance & capstone (Labs 12–14) | Add safety, approval, evaluation and rollback; plug in domain specialists; integrate everything into a deployed, team-owned system.


=== TABLE 46 ===
Capability | What it means
The | The Notes Repository Agent keeps one current, versioned set of materials organized by unit/topic.
The | The Assignment Distribution Agent releases assignments on schedule and sends deadline reminders.
The | The Submission Collection Agent gathers student submissions and timestamps them against the deadline.
The | The Plagiarism/Similarity Agent checks each submission and flags high-similarity cases for review.
The | The Grading Assistance Agent drafts a grade and feedback against the rubric for faculty to review.
The | The Project Milestone Tracking Agent checks each group's progress against defined checkpoints.


=== TABLE 47 ===
# | Agent | Role & communication | Guardrails
0 | apniLeap AURA Coordinator | Sets up the course structure, initializes agents, monitors state across all three streams. | Never publish a grade without faculty approval; enforce access scope per role.
1 | Notes Repository Agent | Maintains one current, versioned set of materials by unit/topic. | Keep clear version history; never remove a prior version without faculty request.
2 | Assignment Distribution Agent | Releases assignments on schedule and sends deadline reminders. | Distribute only faculty-approved assignment briefs and rubrics.
3 | Submission Collection Agent | Gathers submissions and timestamps them against the deadline. | Record late submissions accurately; never alter a submission timestamp.
4 | Plagiarism / Similarity Agent | Checks submissions for similarity and flags high-similarity cases. | Flag for human review; never make a final plagiarism determination automatically.
5 | Grading Assistance Agent | Drafts a grade and feedback against the rubric. | Draft only; never publish a grade without faculty approval.
6 | Project Milestone Tracking Agent | Tracks each group's progress against defined checkpoints. | Report status only from submitted evidence; never assume progress not shown.
7 | Faculty Review | Reviews drafted grades, feedback, and similarity flags; approves or edits. | Faculty decision is final authority on every grade and feedback comment.
8 | Grade Publishing & Archive Agent | Publishes approved grades/feedback and archives the submission record. | Keep a full audit log of grades published and by whom approved.

From | To | Message / payload | When
Coordinator | Notes Repository Agent | run-context + task | run start
Notes Repository Agent | Assignment Distribution Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Assignment Distribution Agent | Submission Collection Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Submission Collection Agent | Plagiarism / Similarity Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Plagiarism / Similarity Agent | Grading Assistance Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Grading Assistance Agent | Project Milestone Tracking Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Project Milestone Tracking Agent | Faculty Review | state / partial result | in pipeline
Faculty Review | Grade Publishing & Archive Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Grade Publishing & Archive Agent | Course Faculty gate | draft organised course workspace (notes, assignments, grades) + evidence | for review
Course Faculty gate | Coordinator | approve | edit | override | human decision
Coordinator | Governance / Publish | final organised course workspace (notes, assignments, grades) (versioned) | on approval


=== TABLE 49 ===
Entity | Key fields
Notes/materials | Lecture notes, slides, and reference material tagged by unit/topic and version.
Assignment definition | Assignment brief, due date, marks, and rubric.
Submission record | Student submission, timestamp, and similarity/plagiarism score.
Project milestones | Defined checkpoints, expected deliverables, and group ownership.
Grade & feedback | Draft grade and feedback pending faculty approval.


=== TABLE 50 ===
function run_course_project_notes_assignment_organize(request):
    ctx = Coordinator.new_run(request)        # isolated, checkpointed
    plan = Coordinator.build_plan(request)     # locked before work begins
    ctx = NotesRepositoryAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = AssignmentDistributionAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = SubmissionCollectionAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = PlagiarismSimilarityAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = GradingAssistanceAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ProjectMilestoneTrackingAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = FacultyReview.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = GradePublishingArchiveAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    report = ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan)   # hard gate
    while not report.passed and attempts < MAX:    # self-healing (Lab 10)
        Coordinator.regenerate(report.failing); report = validate(ctx, plan)
    draft = assemble(ctx)                          # the organised course workspace (notes, assignments, grades)
    decision = CourseGate.review(draft)  # HUMAN — final authority
    if decision.approved:
        final = apply(decision.edits, draft)
        Governance.publish(final, version=next()); Audit.log_all()
    return final


=== TABLE 51 ===
function ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan):
    checks = [
      ('a draft grade never exceeds the rubric maximum', test_0(ctx, plan)),
      ('late submissions are flagged accurately', test_1(ctx, plan)),
      ('a plagiarism/similarity score is attached', test_2(ctx, plan)),
      ('no grade is published without faculty approval', test_3(ctx, plan)),
    ]
    failing = [n for n,ok in checks if not ok]
    return Report(passed = not failing, failing = failing,
                  diagnosis = explain(failing))


=== TABLE 52 ===
connector.expose:
    read('the notes/materials store')
    read('assignment & rubric definitions')
    read('submission records')
agents.use(connector)   # no agent touches a source directly


=== TABLE 53 ===
Stage | What happens
Plan / slot | The coordinator locks the plan and picks one assignment submission to work — its constraints fixed before any content is chosen.
Source candidate | A retrieval agent proposes a candidate for this assignment submission from the notes/materials store, with provenance.
Grounded candidate | A generation agent produces a fresh candidate grounded in the faculty-approved rubric and syllabus, attaching an evidence record (source, scope, tags).
Screen & select | Candidates are calibrated and screened; the best-aligned one that satisfies the plan is selected, the rest logged.
Validation | The result is checked (a draft grade never exceeds the rubric maximum; a plagiarism/similarity score is attached); on failure only this assignment submission is regenerated.
Human review | Course Faculty sees the draft organised course workspace (notes, assignments, grades) with its evidence and validation report, and approves, edits or overrides.
Publish & audit | On approval the governed publish versions the organised course workspace (notes, assignments, grades), records the audit trail, and updates history for future runs.


=== TABLE 54 ===
Stage | Execution mode & coordinator behavior
Gate 1: course setup | Sequential — notes, assignment briefs, and milestones must be faculty-approved before release.
Parallel distribution | Parallel — Notes Repository Agent and Assignment Distribution Agent operate independently.
Parallel collection | Parallel — Submission Collection Agent and Plagiarism/Similarity Agent process each submission as it arrives.
Grading draft | Sequential per submission — Grading Assistance Agent drafts only after the similarity check completes.
Faculty approval | Sequential — grades and feedback are approved before publishing.
Milestone tracking | Parallel, ongoing — runs independently of the assignment grading cycle.


=== TABLE 55 ===
Panel | Shows
Notes portal | Current, versioned materials by unit/topic with version history.
Submission dashboard | On-time/late status and similarity flags per student.
Draft grades queue | Rubric-aligned draft grades and feedback awaiting faculty approval.
Milestone tracker | Each project group's checkpoint status and schedule health.

=== TABLE 91 ===
# | Agent | Role & communication | Guardrails
0 | apniLeap AURA Coordinator | Watches connected folders, creates the run plan, initializes agents, monitors state. | Never auto-send; enforce inbox access scope and audit logging.
1 | Ingestion & Classification Agent | Tags each email by sender category and content type. | Never expose email content beyond the faculty's own access scope.
2 | Priority & Urgency Agent | Ranks emails by deadline signals and escalation language. | Flag uncertain urgency for faculty judgment rather than guessing.
3 | Draft Reply Agent | Proposes a reply using the standard-response library where applicable. | Draft only; never send without explicit faculty approval.
4 | Action-Item Extraction Agent | Extracts tasks, deadlines, and commitments into a tracked list. | Do not mark an item complete without faculty or task-owner confirmation.
5 | Follow-up / Reminder Agent | Resurfaces “reply later” emails on a faculty-set schedule. | Never let a flagged item silently expire without a reminder.
6 | Calendar & Task Sync Agent | Matches meeting requests to the calendar and proposes slots. | Never confirm a meeting time without faculty approval.
7 | Faculty Review | Reviews the draft queue, approves, edits, or discards. | Faculty decision is final authority for every outgoing reply.
8 | Archive & Logging Agent | Sends approved replies, logs the exchange, archives resolved items. | Keep a full audit log of every reply sent and by whom it was approved.

Action-Item Extraction Agent | Follow-up / Reminder Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Follow-up / Reminder Agent | Calendar & Task Sync Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Calendar & Task Sync Agent | Faculty Review | state / partial result | in pipeline
Faculty Review | Archive & Logging Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Archive & Logging Agent | The Mailbox Owner gate | draft triaged inbox with draft replies + evidence | for review
The Mailbox Owner gate | Coordinator | approve | edit | override | human decision
Coordinator | Governance / Publish | final triaged inbox with draft replies (versioned) | on approval


=== TABLE 93 ===
Entity | Key fields
Sender category | Student, admin, collaborator, vendor, or other — used for routing and priority.
Urgency signal | Deadline mentions, escalation language, or explicit priority markers in the email.
Standard response library | Faculty's existing template replies for common questions.
Action-item pattern | Phrases indicating a task, deadline, or commitment embedded in the email body.
Meeting request signal | Proposed dates/times or scheduling language within the email.


=== TABLE 94 ===
function run_email_organizer_agent(request):
    ctx = Coordinator.new_run(request)        # isolated, checkpointed
    plan = Coordinator.build_plan(request)     # locked before work begins
    ctx = IngestionClassificationAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = PriorityUrgencyAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = DraftReplyAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ActionItemExtractionAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = FollowupReminderAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = CalendarTaskSyncAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = FacultyReview.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ArchiveLoggingAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    report = ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan)   # hard gate
    while not report.passed and attempts < MAX:    # self-healing (Lab 10)
        Coordinator.regenerate(report.failing); report = validate(ctx, plan)
    draft = assemble(ctx)                          # the triaged inbox with draft replies
    decision = TheGate.review(draft)  # HUMAN — final authority
    if decision.approved:
        final = apply(decision.edits, draft)
        Governance.publish(final, version=next()); Audit.log_all()
    return final


=== TABLE 95 ===
function ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan):
    checks = [
      ('no email is sent without the owner's approval', test_0(ctx, plan)),
      ('each draft reply cites its thread context', test_1(ctx, plan)),
      ('priority matches the owner's rules', test_2(ctx, plan)),
      ('no mailbox data leaves the account', test_3(ctx, plan)),
    ]
    failing = [n for n,ok in checks if not ok]
    return Report(passed = not failing, failing = failing,
                  diagnosis = explain(failing))

    read('the inbox & threads')
    read('contacts & prior replies')
    read('the calendar & task list')
agents.use(connector)   # no agent touches a source directly


=== TABLE 97 ===
Stage | What happens
Plan / slot | The coordinator locks the plan and picks one email thread to work — its constraints fixed before any content is chosen.
Source candidate | A retrieval agent proposes a candidate for this email thread from the inbox & threads, with provenance.
Grounded candidate | A generation agent produces a fresh candidate grounded in the actual thread history and the owner's past replies, attaching an evidence record (source, scope, tags).
Screen & select | Candidates are calibrated and screened; the best-aligned one that satisfies the plan is selected, the rest logged.
Validation | The result is checked (no email is sent without the owner's approval; priority matches the owner's rules); on failure only this email thread is regenerated.
Human review | The Mailbox Owner sees the draft triaged inbox with draft replies with its evidence and validation report, and approves, edits or overrides.
Publish & audit | On approval the governed publish versions the triaged inbox with draft replies, records the audit trail, and updates history for future runs.


=== TABLE 98 ===
Stage | Execution mode & coordinator behavior
Gate 1: ingestion | Sequential — classification must complete before priority ranking or drafting begins.
Parallel triage | Parallel — Priority Agent, Action-Item Agent, and Calendar Sync Agent all work off the classified inbox simultaneously.
Draft generation | Parallel — Draft Reply Agent works across all queued emails independently.
Faculty approval | Sequential — nothing is sent until faculty reviews the draft queue.
Send and archive | Sequential after approval — Archive & Logging Agent sends and logs each approved reply in turn.
Follow-up scheduling | Parallel, ongoing — runs independently of the daily triage cycle.


=== TABLE 99 ===
Panel | Shows
Triaged inbox | Emails ranked by sender category and urgency.
Draft-reply queue | Proposed replies awaiting faculty approval.
Action-item list | Tasks, deadlines, and commitments extracted from mail.
Follow-up digest | “Reply later” items resurfacing on schedule, plus response-time metrics.

=== TABLE 128 ===
Order | Capability | What it delivers | Agents involved
1 | Ingestion & classification | Tag and categorize incoming mail. | Coordinator, Ingestion & Classification Agent.

3 | Action-item extraction | Pull tasks/deadlines into a tracked list. | Action-Item Extraction Agent.

=== TABLE 129 ===
Risk | Why it matters | Mitigation (where handled)
Plan drift | individual results deviate from the locked plan | Lock the plan (Lab 3/7); validation rejects any deviation before the human sees it.
Ungrounded output | content not backed by the actual thread history and the owner's past replies | Grounded generation with an evidence record per unit (Lab 9); ungrounded items are rejected.
Failed checks slip through | a result violates a rule (no email is sent without the owner's approval) | Validation is a hard gate (Lab 10/11); failing results are diagnosed and regenerated, never advanced.
Runaway loops | the regenerate loop never converges | Bound MAX_ATTEMPTS (Lab 7/10); on exhaustion, escalate to the mailbox owner with a diagnosis.
Over-automation | the system acts without the mailbox owner | The human gate is a hard stop; all outputs are drafts; edits and overrides are effortless and logged (Lab 12).

Week | Labs | Focus | Exit deliverable
Week 1 | Labs 1–3 | Planner loop, tools, reusable skills | Runnable planner + core skill
Week 2 | Labs 4–6 | Memory/retrieval, connector, governed runtime | Data via connector; audited runs
Week 3 | Labs 7–8 | Full node graph + parallel processing | End-to-end draft — shared with faculty
Week 4 | Labs 9–10 | Grounding + self-healing validation | Grounded output; auto-repair on faults
Week 5 | Labs 11–12 | Tested build + safety/governance | Tested artifact; approval, metrics, rollback
Week 6 | Labs 13–14 | Domain specialisation + capstone | Deployed system + demo on a real case

=== TABLE 132 ===
Term | Meaning
Coordinator | The orchestrator agent that owns run-state, plan, budgets and the audit log.
Node graph | The explicit wiring of agents, gates and back-edges for one worklet.
Human gate | The approval step; the named human is the final authority.
Grounding | Linking an output to a real in-scope source (evidence).
Validation | The automated acceptance gate a result must pass before human review.
Blueprint / plan | The locked specification the pipeline obeys.


=== TABLE 133 ===
Phase | What it establishes
Phase A — Foundations (Labs 1–6) | Turn a single agent loop into a tool-using, skill-driven, memory-backed agent that reaches all data through one connector and runs inside a governed runtime with an audit log.
Phase B — Orchestration (Labs 7–8) | Wire the specialist agents into an explicit node graph, then parallelise the per-unit work and merge the best results.
Phase C — Applied reliability (Labs 9–11) | Ground every output in evidence, make the pipeline self-healing on validation failure, and treat the spec as a tested build.
Phase D — Governance & capstone (Labs 12–14) | Add safety, approval, evaluation and rollback; plug in domain specialists; integrate everything into a deployed, team-owned system.


=== TABLE 134 ===
Capability | What it means
The | The Scheduling & Availability Agent proposes common slots across participant calendars.
The | The Agenda Preparation Agent drafts a structured agenda from the stated purpose and carried-over action items.
The | The Resource & Room Booking Agent reserves the room, video link, or equipment needed.
During | During or after the meeting, the Transcription & Minutes Agent drafts structured minutes from notes or a transcript.
The | The Action-Item Tracker Agent extracts new action items with owners and due dates, and carries forward open ones.
The | The Follow-up Reminder Agent nudges owners of open action items ahead of the next meeting.


=== TABLE 135 ===
# | Agent | Role & communication | Guardrails
0 | apniLeap AURA Coordinator | Creates the run plan for a meeting, initializes agents, monitors state through to close-out. | Never finalize minutes without faculty approval; enforce audit logging.
1 | Scheduling & Availability Agent | Proposes common slots across participant calendars. | Never confirm a slot without participant/faculty approval.
2 | Agenda Preparation Agent | Drafts a structured agenda from the stated purpose and carried-over items. | Carry forward all open action items; never drop one silently.
3 | Transcription & Minutes Agent | Drafts structured minutes from notes, transcript, or dictation. | Draft only from the actual record; never invent discussion content.
4 | Action-Item Tracker Agent | Extracts action items with owner and due date; tracks status across meetings. | Never mark an item complete without owner or faculty confirmation.
5 | Follow-up Reminder Agent | Nudges action-item owners ahead of the next meeting. | Never let an open item silently expire without a reminder.
6 | Resource & Room Booking Agent | Reserves room, video link, or equipment. | Confirm bookings only within faculty-authorized resources.
7 | Faculty Review | Reviews and approves the final minutes and action-item list. | Faculty decision is final authority on the official meeting record.
8 | Distribution & Logging Agent | Circulates approved minutes and updates the dashboard. | Keep full version history of agenda and minutes drafts.

=== TABLE 137 ===

=== TABLE 138 ===
function run_meeting_organizer_agent(request):
    ctx = Coordinator.new_run(request)        # isolated, checkpointed
    plan = Coordinator.build_plan(request)     # locked before work begins
    ctx = SchedulingAvailabilityAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = AgendaPreparationAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = TranscriptionMinutesAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ActionItemTrackerAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = FollowupReminderAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ResourceRoomBookingAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = FacultyReview.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = DistributionLoggingAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    report = ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan)   # hard gate
    while not report.passed and attempts < MAX:    # self-healing (Lab 10)
        Coordinator.regenerate(report.failing); report = validate(ctx, plan)
    draft = assemble(ctx)                          # the scheduled meeting with agenda and minutes
    decision = TheGate.review(draft)  # HUMAN — final authority
    if decision.approved:
        final = apply(decision.edits, draft)
        Governance.publish(final, version=next()); Audit.log_all()
    return final


=== TABLE 139 ===
function ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan):
    checks = [
      ('no invite is sent without the owner's approval', test_0(ctx, plan)),
      ('the slot respects every stated constraint', test_1(ctx, plan)),
      ('the agenda covers all required items', test_2(ctx, plan)),
      ('minutes cite decisions and owners', test_3(ctx, plan)),
    ]
    failing = [n for n,ok in checks if not ok]
    return Report(passed = not failing, failing = failing,
                  diagnosis = explain(failing))

    read('calendars & availability')
    read('the invitee list & preferences')
    read('agenda/notes documents')
agents.use(connector)   # no agent touches a source directly


=== TABLE 141 ===
Stage | What happens
Plan / slot | The coordinator locks the plan and picks one meeting to work — its constraints fixed before any content is chosen.
Source candidate | A retrieval agent proposes a candidate for this meeting from calendars & availability, with provenance.
Grounded candidate | A generation agent produces a fresh candidate grounded in participant availability and prior meeting notes, attaching an evidence record (source, scope, tags).
Screen & select | Candidates are calibrated and screened; the best-aligned one that satisfies the plan is selected, the rest logged.
Validation | The result is checked (no invite is sent without the owner's approval; the agenda covers all required items); on failure only this meeting is regenerated.
Human review | The Meeting Owner sees the draft scheduled meeting with agenda and minutes with its evidence and validation report, and approves, edits or overrides.
Publish & audit | On approval the governed publish versions the scheduled meeting with agenda and minutes, records the audit trail, and updates history for future runs.


=== TABLE 142 ===
Stage | Execution mode & coordinator behavior
Gate 1: purpose and participants | Sequential — scheduling starts only once purpose and participant list are confirmed.
Parallel preparation | Parallel — Agenda Agent drafts the agenda while Resource & Room Booking Agent reserves logistics.
During/after meeting | Sequential — minutes drafting begins only once the meeting record (notes/transcript) is available.
Parallel extraction | Parallel — Action-Item Tracker Agent and Distribution Agent prepare drafts once minutes are ready.
Faculty approval | Sequential — minutes and action items are approved together before circulation.
Follow-up | Parallel, ongoing — reminders run independently until the next meeting.

=== TABLE 173 ===
Risk | Why it matters | Mitigation (where handled)
Plan drift | individual results deviate from the locked plan | Lock the plan (Lab 3/7); validation rejects any deviation before the human sees it.
Ungrounded output | content not backed by participant availability and prior meeting notes | Grounded generation with an evidence record per unit (Lab 9); ungrounded items are rejected.
Failed checks slip through | a result violates a rule (no invite is sent without the owner's approval) | Validation is a hard gate (Lab 10/11); failing results are diagnosed and regenerated, never advanced.
Runaway loops | the regenerate loop never converges | Bound MAX_ATTEMPTS (Lab 7/10); on exhaustion, escalate to the meeting owner with a diagnosis.
Over-automation | the system acts without the meeting owner | The human gate is a hard stop; all outputs are drafts; edits and overrides are effortless and logged (Lab 12).

=== TABLE 177 ===
Phase | What it establishes
Phase A — Foundations (Labs 1–6) | Turn a single agent loop into a tool-using, skill-driven, memory-backed agent that reaches all data through one connector and runs inside a governed runtime with an audit log.
Phase B — Orchestration (Labs 7–8) | Wire the specialist agents into an explicit node graph, then parallelise the per-unit work and merge the best results.
Phase C — Applied reliability (Labs 9–11) | Ground every output in evidence, make the pipeline self-healing on validation failure, and treat the spec as a tested build.
Phase D — Governance & capstone (Labs 12–14) | Add safety, approval, evaluation and rollback; plug in domain specialists; integrate everything into a deployed, team-owned system.


=== TABLE 178 ===

The | The department links the current curriculum, course outcomes, and credit structure, along with industry-partner and alumni feedback channels.
The | The Curriculum Mapping, Industry Skills, and ACM/APNI Benchmarking agents each build their respective view in parallel.
The | The Gap Analysis Agent compares the three views to identify missing topics, outdated content, and skill mismatches.
The | The Recommendation Agent proposes specific revisions — new courses/electives, updated outcomes, topics to add or retire — tied to each identified gap.
HoD/Dean | HoD/Dean (Academics)/Board of Studies reviews the gap analysis and recommendations, approves or adjusts them, and approved revisions are released for curriculum adoption.


=== TABLE 179 ===
# | Agent | Role & communication | Guardrails
0 | apniLeap AURA Coordinator | Receives the faculty/HoD prompt command, checks permissions, creates the run plan, initializes agents, and monitors state. | Never bypass human approval; enforce data scope, audit logging, source citation, and exception routing.
1 | Curriculum Mapping Agent | Maps courses, outcomes, credit structure, and unit content into a structured curriculum model, in parallel with B and C. | Use only the approved curriculum document; never infer an outcome not stated; flag outdated or missing sections.
2 | Industry Skills Agent | Aggregates industry-demanded skills from job postings and industry/alumni feedback, in parallel with A and C. | Pull only from authorized sources; cite source and date per trend; flag low-confidence or single-source trends.
3 | ACM/APNI Benchmarking Agent | Benchmarks the curriculum against ACM curricula guidelines and the APNI standard, in parallel with A and B. | Use only the current approved version; cite the clause behind every comparison; never claim compliance without a matched clause.
4 | Gap Analysis Agent | Compares the three views to identify missing topics, outdated content, and skill mismatches with severity and evidence. | Tie every gap to specific evidence; never invent a gap; flag conflicting signals for human interpretation.
5 | Recommendation Agent | Proposes specific revisions tied to each identified gap. | Tie every recommendation to a gap and evidence; never propose changes outside approved program scope; flag high-impact changes.
6 | HoD / Dean / Board of Studies Review | Reviews the gap analysis and recommendations, approves, adjusts, or rejects, and releases approved changes. | Board of Studies decision is the final authority for adopting a curriculum revision.

=== TABLE 182 ===
function run_curriculum_intelligence_system(request):
    ctx = Coordinator.new_run(request)        # isolated, checkpointed
    plan = Coordinator.build_plan(request)     # locked before work begins
    ctx = CurriculumMappingAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = IndustrySkillsAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ACMAPNIBenchmarkingAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = GapAnalysisAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = RecommendationAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = HoDDeanBoardofStudiesReview.step(ctx, plan)
    report = ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan)   # hard gate
    while not report.passed and attempts < MAX:    # self-healing (Lab 10)
        Coordinator.regenerate(report.failing); report = validate(ctx, plan)
    draft = assemble(ctx)                          # the curriculum revision recommendation
    decision = TheGate.review(draft)  # HUMAN — final authority
    if decision.approved:
        final = apply(decision.edits, draft)
        Governance.publish(final, version=next()); Audit.log_all()
    return final


=== TABLE 183 ===
function ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan):
    checks = [
      ('every recommendation cites its evidence', test_0(ctx, plan)),
      ('CO/PO mapping stays consistent', test_1(ctx, plan)),
      ('no change is published without committee appro', test_2(ctx, plan)),
      ('the gap analysis is reproducible', test_3(ctx, plan)),
    ]
    failing = [n for n,ok in checks if not ok]
    return Report(passed = not failing, failing = failing,
                  diagnosis = explain(failing))

=== TABLE 185 ===
Stage | What happens
Plan / slot | The coordinator locks the plan and picks one course / CO to work — its constraints fixed before any content is chosen.
Source candidate | A retrieval agent proposes a candidate for this course / CO from syllabi & CO/PO maps, with provenance.
Grounded candidate | A generation agent produces a fresh candidate grounded in accreditation criteria and outcome-attainment data, attaching an evidence record (source, scope, tags).
Screen & select | Candidates are calibrated and screened; the best-aligned one that satisfies the plan is selected, the rest logged.
Validation | The result is checked (every recommendation cites its evidence; no change is published without committee approval); on failure only this course / CO is regenerated.
Human review | The Curriculum Committee / Hod sees the draft curriculum revision recommendation with its evidence and validation report, and approves, edits or overrides.
Publish & audit | On approval the governed publish versions the curriculum revision recommendation, records the audit trail, and updates history for future runs.


=== TABLE 186 ===
Stage | Execution mode & coordinator behavior
Gate 1: curriculum intake | Sequential — start only after the curriculum, outcomes, and industry/alumni feedback channels are linked.
Parallel view-building | Parallel — Curriculum Mapping, Industry Skills, and ACM/APNI Benchmarking agents each build their view at the same time.
Gap analysis | Sequential — Gap Analysis Agent runs only after all three views are available.
Recommendation | Sequential — Recommendation Agent proposes revisions only for confirmed gaps.
Stakeholder approval | Sequential — Board of Studies approves or adjusts; corrections are routed back to the relevant agent.
Dashboard update | Parallel after approval — the curriculum intelligence dashboard is published once approved.

=== TABLE 223 ===
# | Agent | Role & communication | Guardrails
0 | apniLeap AURA Coordinator | Receives the faculty/COE prompt command, checks permissions, creates the run plan, initializes agents, and monitors state. | Never bypass human approval; enforce data scope, audit logging, source citation, and exception routing.
1 | Question Bank Agent | Retrieves candidate questions from the approved tagged bank (unit, CO, Bloom's, difficulty, marks, last-used). | Pull only from the approved, faculty-vetted bank; never invent a question; flag questions used in the last two cycles.
2 | Blueprint Agent | Loads the approved paper blueprint — unit weightage, marks pattern, question-type structure, Bloom's targets. | Use only the officially approved blueprint; never modify a requirement without sign-off; flag a missing/outdated blueprint.
3 | Bloom's Taxonomy Agent | Confirms or tags each candidate question's cognitive level. | Use only the approved rubric; flag ambiguous questions; never reword a question to force a fit.
4 | Question Selection Agent | Assembles a compliant set matching unit/marks/Bloom's targets, avoiding recent repeats. | Never select a flagged repeat without explicit override; show which requirement each question satisfies; flag unfillable requirements.
5 | CO-PO Mapping Agent | Maps each selected question to its CO(s) and linked PO(s) via the articulation matrix. | Use only the approved CO list and CO-PO matrix; never infer a link not in the matrix; flag questions that do not map cleanly.
6 | Difficulty & Coverage Agent | Scores difficulty and marks-time balance and validates CO/unit and Bloom's coverage against the blueprint. | Base scores on the approved rubric; flag under/over-represented COs and units; never approve coverage with an unresolved gap.
7 | Quality & Validation Agent | Checks wording, formatting, duplicates, and blueprint deviations; generates the scheme of valuation. | Flag issues rather than editing; generate the scheme only from questions in the paper; route mismatches to faculty.
8 | Faculty / HoD / COE / IQAC Review | Reviews the paper, OBE analysis, CO-PO tags, and scheme; approves or requests corrections; releases for printing. | Faculty/HoD/COE/IQAC decision is the final authority for releasing a paper and its CO-PO attainment data.

=== TABLE 226 ===
function run_question_paper_setting_obe_analysis_syst(request):
    ctx = Coordinator.new_run(request)        # isolated, checkpointed
    plan = Coordinator.build_plan(request)     # locked before work begins
    ctx = QuestionBankAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = BlueprintAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = BloomsTaxonomyAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = QuestionSelectionAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = COPOMappingAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = DifficultyCoverageAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = QualityValidationAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = FacultyHoDCOEIQACReview.step(ctx, plan)
    report = ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan)   # hard gate
    while not report.passed and attempts < MAX:    # self-healing (Lab 10)
        Coordinator.regenerate(report.failing); report = validate(ctx, plan)
    draft = assemble(ctx)                          # the question paper and OBE analysis
    decision = TheGate.review(draft)  # HUMAN — final authority
    if decision.approved:
        final = apply(decision.edits, draft)
        Governance.publish(final, version=next()); Audit.log_all()
    return final


=== TABLE 227 ===
function ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan):
    checks = [
      ('marks total equals the paper maximum', test_0(ctx, plan)),
      ('every unit and CO is covered', test_1(ctx, plan)),
      ('no repeats against the last N papers', test_2(ctx, plan)),
      ('each answer-key item sums to its marks', test_3(ctx, plan)),
    ]
    failing = [n for n,ok in checks if not ok]
    return Report(passed = not failing, failing = failing,
                  diagnosis = explain(failing))

    read('the syllabus & CO/PO map')
    read('past-paper history')

=== TABLE 229 ===
Stage | What happens
Plan / slot | The coordinator locks the plan and picks one question slot / student to work — its constraints fixed before any content is chosen.
Source candidate | A retrieval agent proposes a candidate for this question slot / student from the question bank, with provenance.
Grounded candidate | A generation agent produces a fresh candidate grounded in the syllabus and prescribed texts, attaching an evidence record (source, scope, tags).
Screen & select | Candidates are calibrated and screened; the best-aligned one that satisfies the plan is selected, the rest logged.
Validation | The result is checked (marks total equals the paper maximum; no repeats against the last N papers); on failure only this question slot / student is regenerated.
Human review | The Faculty Setter & Coe sees the draft question paper and OBE analysis with its evidence and validation report, and approves, edits or overrides.
Publish & audit | On approval the governed publish versions the question paper and OBE analysis, records the audit trail, and updates history for future runs.


=== TABLE 230 ===
Stage | Execution mode & coordinator behavior
Gate 1: bank & blueprint intake | Sequential — start only after the approved bank, blueprint, CO list, and CO-PO matrix are loaded.
Parallel retrieval | Parallel — Question Bank Agent retrieves candidates while Blueprint Agent loads structural requirements.
Bloom's tagging | Sequential — tags confirmed after the candidate pool is returned.
Selection | Sequential — the paper is assembled once Bloom's tags and blueprint requirements are both available.
Parallel analysis | Parallel — CO-PO mapping and difficulty/coverage analysis run together on the assembled paper.
Validation & scheme generation | Sequential — quality validation and the scheme of valuation follow the analysis.
Faculty approval | Sequential — faculty/HoD/COE/IQAC approves the paper, tags, and scheme; corrections route back.
Dashboard update | Parallel after approval — paper status, blueprint compliance, OBE analysis, and scheme are published together.

Quality & scheme | Quality exceptions and the generated scheme of valuation, with approval status.

=== TABLE 260 ===
Order | Capability | What it delivers | Agents involved
1 | Bank & blueprint intake | Load tagged bank, blueprint, CO list, CO-PO matrix. | Coordinator, Question Bank & Blueprint Agents.
2 | Bloom's tagging & selection | Tag levels; assemble a compliant, repeat-aware paper. | Bloom's Taxonomy, Question Selection Agents.
3 | CO-PO mapping | Map questions to COs and linked POs. | CO-PO Mapping Agent.
4 | Difficulty & coverage analysis | Difficulty/balance and blueprint coverage checks. | Difficulty & Coverage Agent.
5 | Quality review & scheme | Quality flags and matching scheme of valuation. | Quality & Validation Agent.
6 | Setting-&-analysis dashboard | Paper, OBE analysis, and scheme in one view. | Coordinator.


=== TABLE 261 ===
Risk | Why it matters | Mitigation (where handled)
Plan drift | individual results deviate from the locked plan | Lock the plan (Lab 3/7); validation rejects any deviation before the human sees it.
Ungrounded output | content not backed by the syllabus and prescribed texts | Grounded generation with an evidence record per unit (Lab 9); ungrounded items are rejected.
Failed checks slip through | a result violates a rule (marks total equals the paper maximum) | Validation is a hard gate (Lab 10/11); failing results are diagnosed and regenerated, never advanced.
Runaway loops | the regenerate loop never converges | Bound MAX_ATTEMPTS (Lab 7/10); on exhaustion, escalate to the faculty setter & CoE with a diagnosis.
Over-automation | the system acts without the faculty setter & CoE | The human gate is a hard stop; all outputs are drafts; edits and overrides are effortless and logged (Lab 12).

Week | Labs | Focus | Exit deliverable
Week 1 | Labs 1–3 | Planner loop, tools, reusable skills | Runnable planner + core skill
Week 2 | Labs 4–6 | Memory/retrieval, connector, governed runtime | Data via connector; audited runs
Week 3 | Labs 7–8 | Full node graph + parallel processing | End-to-end draft — shared with faculty
Week 4 | Labs 9–10 | Grounding + self-healing validation | Grounded output; auto-repair on faults
Week 5 | Labs 11–12 | Tested build + safety/governance | Tested artifact; approval, metrics, rollback
Week 6 | Labs 13–14 | Domain specialisation + capstone | Deployed system + demo on a real case

=== TABLE 264 ===
Term | Meaning
Coordinator | The orchestrator agent that owns run-state, plan, budgets and the audit log.
Node graph | The explicit wiring of agents, gates and back-edges for one worklet.
Human gate | The approval step; the named human is the final authority.
Grounding | Linking an output to a real in-scope source (evidence).
Validation | The automated acceptance gate a result must pass before human review.
Blueprint / plan | The locked specification the pipeline obeys.


=== TABLE 265 ===
Phase | What it establishes
Phase A — Foundations (Labs 1–6) | Turn a single agent loop into a tool-using, skill-driven, memory-backed agent that reaches all data through one connector and runs inside a governed runtime with an audit log.
Phase B — Orchestration (Labs 7–8) | Wire the specialist agents into an explicit node graph, then parallelise the per-unit work and merge the best results.
Phase C — Applied reliability (Labs 9–11) | Ground every output in evidence, make the pipeline self-healing on validation failure, and treat the spec as a tested build.
Phase D — Governance & capstone (Labs 12–14) | Add safety, approval, evaluation and rollback; plug in domain specialists; integrate everything into a deployed, team-owned system.


=== TABLE 266 ===
Capability | What it means
The | The Registration & Eligibility Agent checks fee status, attendance, and prerequisite clearance before allowing registration.
The | The Exam Delivery Agent releases the exam within the defined delivery window and manages session access.
The | The Proctoring & Integrity Agent monitors session activity and flags suspected integrity issues for review.
The | The Auto-Grading Agent grades objective questions immediately against the answer key.
The | The Subjective Grading Assist Agent drafts grades for descriptive answers against the rubric.
Faculty | Faculty reviews integrity flags and subjective grading drafts, approving or adjusting each.


=== TABLE 267 ===
# | Agent | Role & communication | Guardrails
0 | apniLeap AURA Coordinator | Sets up the exam run, initializes agents, monitors state through to result publishing. | Never publish results without faculty approval; enforce audit logging.
1 | Registration & Eligibility Agent | Checks fee status, attendance, and prerequisite clearance before registration. | Apply eligibility rules consistently; flag ambiguous cases for human decision.
2 | Exam Delivery Agent | Releases the exam within the delivery window and manages session access. | Enforce the defined window; never extend or restart a session without faculty approval.
3 | Proctoring & Integrity Agent | Monitors session activity and flags suspected integrity issues. | Flag only; never apply an automatic penalty or disqualification.
4 | Auto-Grading Agent (Objective) | Grades objective questions immediately against the answer key. | Grade only against the approved key; flag key mismatches rather than guessing.
5 | Subjective Grading Assist Agent | Drafts grades for descriptive answers against the rubric. | Draft only; never publish a subjective grade without faculty approval.
6 | Grievance & Revaluation Agent | Tracks post-result requests through to resolution. | Log every request and its resolution; never resolve a grievance automatically.
7 | Faculty Review | Reviews integrity flags and subjective grading drafts; approves or adjusts. | Faculty decision is final authority for grades and integrity outcomes.
8 | Result Publishing & Archive Agent | Publishes approved results and archives the exam record. | Keep a full audit log of the exam session, grading, and publishing steps.


=== TABLE 268 ===
From | To | Message / payload | When
Coordinator | Registration & Eligibility Agent | run-context + task | run start
Registration & Eligibility Agent | Exam Delivery Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Exam Delivery Agent | Proctoring & Integrity Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Proctoring & Integrity Agent | Auto-Grading Agent (Objective) | state / partial result | in pipeline
Auto-Grading Agent (Objective) | Subjective Grading Assist Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Subjective Grading Assist Agent | Grievance & Revaluation Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Grievance & Revaluation Agent | Faculty Review | state / partial result | in pipeline
Faculty Review | Result Publishing & Archive Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline
Result Publishing & Archive Agent | The Faculty & Coe gate | draft administered online exam with results + evidence | for review
The Faculty & Coe gate | Coordinator | approve | edit | override | human decision
Coordinator | Governance / Publish | final administered online exam with results (versioned) | on approval


=== TABLE 269 ===
Entity | Key fields
Exam definition | Course, question paper, duration, delivery window, and grading rules.
Student eligibility | Fee status, attendance, and prerequisite clearance per student.
Submission & timing log | Answer submissions with timestamps and session activity for integrity checks.
Objective answer key | Correct answers for auto-gradable question types.
Subjective rubric | Marking scheme and key points for descriptive answers.


=== TABLE 270 ===
function run_online_course_examination_system_agent(request):
    ctx = Coordinator.new_run(request)        # isolated, checkpointed
    plan = Coordinator.build_plan(request)     # locked before work begins
    ctx = RegistrationEligibilityAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ExamDeliveryAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ProctoringIntegrityAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = AutoGradingAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = SubjectiveGradingAssistAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = GrievanceRevaluationAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = FacultyReview.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ResultPublishingArchiveAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    report = ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan)   # hard gate
    while not report.passed and attempts < MAX:    # self-healing (Lab 10)
        Coordinator.regenerate(report.failing); report = validate(ctx, plan)
    draft = assemble(ctx)                          # the administered online exam with results
    decision = TheGate.review(draft)  # HUMAN — final authority
    if decision.approved:
        final = apply(decision.edits, draft)
        Governance.publish(final, version=next()); Audit.log_all()
    return final


=== TABLE 271 ===
function ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan):
    checks = [
      ('each candidate gets a unique paper', test_0(ctx, plan)),
      ('integrity signals are logged, not turned into ', test_1(ctx, plan)),
      ('an auto-grade never exceeds the maximum', test_2(ctx, plan)),
      ('results are held until faculty sign-off', test_3(ctx, plan)),
    ]
    failing = [n for n,ok in checks if not ok]
    return Report(passed = not failing, failing = failing,
                  diagnosis = explain(failing))


=== TABLE 272 ===
connector.expose:
    read('the question pool')
    read('the candidate roster & eligibility')
    read('proctoring signals')
agents.use(connector)   # no agent touches a source directly


=== TABLE 273 ===
Stage | What happens
Plan / slot | The coordinator locks the plan and picks one candidate attempt to work — its constraints fixed before any content is chosen.
Source candidate | A retrieval agent proposes a candidate for this candidate attempt from the question pool, with provenance.
Grounded candidate | A generation agent produces a fresh candidate grounded in the approved question pool and rubric, attaching an evidence record (source, scope, tags).
Screen & select | Candidates are calibrated and screened; the best-aligned one that satisfies the plan is selected, the rest logged.
Validation | The result is checked (each candidate gets a unique paper; an auto-grade never exceeds the maximum); on failure only this candidate attempt is regenerated.
Human review | The Faculty & Coe sees the draft administered online exam with results with its evidence and validation report, and approves, edits or overrides.
Publish & audit | On approval the governed publish versions the administered online exam with results, records the audit trail, and updates history for future runs.


=== TABLE 274 ===
Stage | Execution mode & coordinator behavior
Gate 1: eligibility | Sequential — registration is confirmed only after eligibility checks pass.
Delivery window | Sequential — the exam opens and closes strictly within the defined window.
Parallel grading | Parallel — Auto-Grading Agent and Proctoring/Integrity Agent process the batch simultaneously as submissions arrive.
Subjective drafting | Parallel across students — Subjective Grading Assist Agent drafts grades once submissions close.
Faculty approval | Sequential — subjective grades and integrity flags are approved before publishing.
Publishing and grievance | Sequential after approval — results publish, then grievance requests are tracked as they arrive.


=== TABLE 275 ===
Panel | Shows
Registration & eligibility | Eligible/registered students with flagged ambiguous cases.
Integrity report | Session flags routed for faculty review.
Grading queue | Objective results and subjective draft grades awaiting approval.
Results & grievances | Published results and tracked revaluation/grievance requests.

Blueprint / plan | The locked specification the pipeline obeys.


=== TABLE 309 ===
Phase | What it establishes
Phase A — Foundations (Labs 1–6) | Turn a single agent loop into a tool-using, skill-driven, memory-backed agent that reaches all data through one connector and runs inside a governed runtime with an audit log.
Phase B — Orchestration (Labs 7–8) | Wire the specialist agents into an explicit node graph, then parallelise the per-unit work and merge the best results.
Phase C — Applied reliability (Labs 9–11) | Ground every output in evidence, make the pipeline self-healing on validation failure, and treat the spec as a tested build.
Phase D — Governance & capstone (Labs 12–14) | Add safety, approval, evaluation and rollback; plug in domain specialists; integrate everything into a deployed, team-owned system.


=== TABLE 310 ===
Capability | What it means
Faculty | Faculty links their research, teaching-learning, and administrative activity records, with explicit consent for each source.
The | The Research, Teaching-Learning, and Administrative Data Collection agents each normalize their respective activity into structured fields.
The | The Evidence Validation Agent checks that every claimed activity has supporting documentation before any score is computed.
The | The API Calculation Agent computes the score using approved weightages, with visible calculation steps.
The | The Report Generation Agent compiles the appraisal report and routes exceptions and low-confidence evidence to a human reviewer.
HoD/Dean/IQAC | HoD/Dean/IQAC reviews exceptions, approves the API score and appraisal report, and releases it to the faculty member.


=== TABLE 311 ===
# | Agent | Role & communication | Guardrails
0 | apniLeap AURA Coordinator | Receives the faculty/HoD prompt command, checks permissions, creates the run plan, initializes agents, and monitors state. | Never bypass human approval; enforce data scope, audit logging, source citation, and exception routing.
1 | Research Data Collection Agent | Collects and normalizes publications, projects, grants, and PhD guidance, in parallel with B and C. | Validate consent before pulling records; never expose unpublished/under-review work beyond permissions; flag incomplete entries.
2 | Teaching-Learning Data Collection Agent | Collects and normalizes teaching load, course feedback, and learning-outcome data, in parallel with A and C. | Pull only from authorized systems; never expose individual student feedback beyond aggregate scores; flag incomplete semesters.
3 | Administrative Data Collection Agent | Collects and normalizes committee roles, event coordination, and institutional duties, in parallel with A and B. | Pull only from authorized records; cite the source office per entry; flag undocumented responsibilities.
4 | Evidence Validation Agent | Cross-checks every claimed activity against its supporting documentation. | Never validate a claim without evidence; flag mismatched/missing documentation; keep an audit trail per claim.
5 | API Calculation Agent | Computes the API score from validated activity using approved weightages. | Use only approved weightage tables; show method and source per component; do not finalize while exceptions are unresolved.
6 | Report Generation Agent | Compiles the appraisal report and dashboard-ready summary. | Publish only HoD/Dean/IQAC-approved data; keep raw self-reported data separate; version every report.
7 | HoD / Dean / IQAC Review | Reviews exceptions and low-confidence evidence, approves the score and report, or sends corrections back. | HoD/Dean/IQAC decision is the final authority for an institution-facing appraisal record.

Teaching-Learning Data Collection Agent | Administrative Data Collection Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline

API Calculation Agent | Report Generation Agent | state / partial result | in pipeline

=== TABLE 313 ===

=== TABLE 314 ===
function run_faculty_appraisal_management_system(request):
    ctx = Coordinator.new_run(request)        # isolated, checkpointed
    plan = Coordinator.build_plan(request)     # locked before work begins
    ctx = ResearchDataCollectionAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = TeachingLearningDataCollectionAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = AdministrativeDataCollectionAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = EvidenceValidationAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = APICalculationAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = ReportGenerationAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = HoDDeanIQACReview.step(ctx, plan)
    report = ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan)   # hard gate
    while not report.passed and attempts < MAX:    # self-healing (Lab 10)
        Coordinator.regenerate(report.failing); report = validate(ctx, plan)
    draft = assemble(ctx)                          # the appraisal summary and rating recommendation
    decision = TheGate.review(draft)  # HUMAN — final authority
    if decision.approved:
        final = apply(decision.edits, draft)
        Governance.publish(final, version=next()); Audit.log_all()
    return final


=== TABLE 315 ===
function ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan):
    checks = [
      ('every score cites its evidence', test_0(ctx, plan)),
      ('weightages match the policy', test_1(ctx, plan)),
      ('no rating is finalised without committee appro', test_2(ctx, plan)),
      ('the appeals trail is preserved', test_3(ctx, plan)),
    ]
    failing = [n for n,ok in checks if not ok]
    return Report(passed = not failing, failing = failing,
                  diagnosis = explain(failing))

=== TABLE 317 ===
Stage | What happens
Plan / slot | The coordinator locks the plan and picks one faculty member / cycle to work — its constraints fixed before any content is chosen.
Source candidate | A retrieval agent proposes a candidate for this faculty member / cycle from teaching/research/service records, with provenance.
Grounded candidate | A generation agent produces a fresh candidate grounded in the approved appraisal rubric and verified records, attaching an evidence record (source, scope, tags).
Screen & select | Candidates are calibrated and screened; the best-aligned one that satisfies the plan is selected, the rest logged.
Validation | The result is checked (every score cites its evidence; no rating is finalised without committee approval); on failure only this faculty member / cycle is regenerated.
Human review | The Hod / Appraisal Committee sees the draft appraisal summary and rating recommendation with its evidence and validation report, and approves, edits or overrides.
Publish & audit | On approval the governed publish versions the appraisal summary and rating recommendation, records the audit trail, and updates history for future runs.


=== TABLE 318 ===
Stage | Execution mode & coordinator behavior
Gate 1: profile intake | Sequential — start only after the faculty links accounts with consent confirmed.
Parallel data collection | Parallel — Research, Teaching-Learning, and Administrative agents each pull records at the same time.
Join and validate | Sequential — Evidence Validation Agent runs after all three collection agents return data.
Scoring | Sequential — API Calculation Agent computes only once validation is complete.
Report assembly | Sequential — the report is compiled from validated evidence and the computed score.
Stakeholder approval | Sequential — HoD/Dean/IQAC approves the score and report; corrections route back.
Dashboard update | Parallel after approval — appraisal dashboard, API score, and evidence status publish together.

=== TABLE 349 ===
Risk | Why it matters | Mitigation (where handled)
Plan drift | individual results deviate from the locked plan | Lock the plan (Lab 3/7); validation rejects any deviation before the human sees it.
Ungrounded output | content not backed by the approved appraisal rubric and verified records | Grounded generation with an evidence record per unit (Lab 9); ungrounded items are rejected.
Failed checks slip through | a result violates a rule (every score cites its evidence) | Validation is a hard gate (Lab 10/11); failing results are diagnosed and regenerated, never advanced.
Runaway loops | the regenerate loop never converges | Bound MAX_ATTEMPTS (Lab 7/10); on exhaustion, escalate to the HoD / appraisal committee with a diagnosis.
Over-automation | the system acts without the HoD / appraisal committee | The human gate is a hard stop; all outputs are drafts; edits and overrides are effortless and logged (Lab 12).

=== TABLE 358 ===
function run_placement_readiness_career_intelligence_(request):
    ctx = Coordinator.new_run(request)        # isolated, checkpointed
    plan = Coordinator.build_plan(request)     # locked before work begins
    ctx = ResumeAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = SkillGapAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = CodingAnalyticsAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = JobMatchingAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = InterviewAgent.step(ctx, plan)
    ctx = PlacementCellConvenerReview.step(ctx, plan)
    report = ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan)   # hard gate
    while not report.passed and attempts < MAX:    # self-healing (Lab 10)
        Coordinator.regenerate(report.failing); report = validate(ctx, plan)
    draft = assemble(ctx)                          # the per-student readiness plan and shortlist
    decision = TheGate.review(draft)  # HUMAN — final authority
    if decision.approved:
        final = apply(decision.edits, draft)
        Governance.publish(final, version=next()); Audit.log_all()
    return final


=== TABLE 359 ===
function ValidationAgent.validate(ctx, plan):
    checks = [
      ('recommendations cite the specific skill gaps', test_0(ctx, plan)),
      ('eligibility rules are enforced', test_1(ctx, plan)),
      ('no application is submitted without the studen', test_2(ctx, plan)),
      ('shortlisting is checked for bias', test_3(ctx, plan)),
    ]
    failing = [n for n,ok in checks if not ok]
    return Report(passed = not failing, failing = failing,
                  diagnosis = explain(failing))
